 `guide receipts`: `useSeen(api.receiptFamilies.chat, { scope, through, active })` owns retries. SDK owns delivery reporting; screen owns visible `seen` reporting. Supply visible confirmed positions; separate retained history/live decorations.
 
 ## One-view photos
-Mount **above the list**; key: `JSON.stringify([backend, account, messageId, assetId])`. Open once; recycling/refetch preserves handles. Unmount/account change revokes late responses. Close on `absent`/denied/tombstone/changed-asset/deadline; deadlines run through loading.
-
-```tsx
-import { useEffect, useRef, useState } from "react";
-import { useClient, useNow, useQuery } from "snapback4/react";
-import { isReady, existence } from "snapback4/contract";
-import { api } from "../snapback/generated/api";
-export function OneView({ messageId, assetId }: { messageId: string; assetId: string }) {
-  const client = useClient(), row = useQuery(api.messagePhoto, { id: messageId });
-  const handle = useRef<{ revoke(): void } | null>(null), alive = useRef(false), deadline = useRef<number | null>(null);
-  const [started, setStarted] = useState(false), [url, setUrl] = useState<string | null>(null), [problem, setProblem] = useState("");
-  const opened = useRef(false);
-  if (isReady(row) && row.existence === "present") deadline.current = row.data.expiresAt;
-  const now = useNow(deadline.current);
-  const closed = existence(row) === "denied" || existence(row) === "absent" || (isReady(row) && row.existence === "present" && (row.data.tombstone || row.data.photo?.id !== assetId)) || (deadline.current !== null && now >= deadline.current);
-  const invalid = useRef(false); invalid.current ||= closed;
-  useEffect(() => { alive.current = true; return () => { alive.current = false; handle.current?.revoke(); }; }, []);
-  useEffect(() => { if (closed) { handle.current?.revoke(); handle.current = null; setUrl(null); } }, [closed]);
-  async function open() {
-    if (opened.current || invalid.current || (!isReady(row) || row.existence !== "present" || row.revalidating)) return;
-    opened.current = true; setStarted(true);
-    const result = await client.asset(assetId);
-    if ("denied" in result) { if (alive.current) setProblem(result.denied.message); return; }
-    if (!alive.current || invalid.current) { result.revoke(); return; }
-    handle.current = result; setUrl(result.url);
-  }
-  return invalid.current ? null : <>{url ? <img src={url} alt="One-view photo" /> : <button disabled={started || !isReady(row) || row.existence !== "present" || row.revalidating} onClick={() => void open()}>Open photo</button>}{problem}</>;
-}
-```
+[One-view photo recipe](CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md): mount above the list; retain handles across refetch; account/unmount revokes late responses; absence/denial/deadline closes.
 `useAsset` revokes on unmount; once media cannot refetch (`E_ASSET_VIEWED`).
# Wire: HTTP for curl, scripts and non-JS clients

Under the JS client is plain JSON over HTTP, which curl, Python, Swift or any HTTP client can speak. Each call below is a POST with a JSON body unless it says GET.

```sh
U=http://127.0.0.1:4400
curl -s -X POST $U/q/mine -H 'x-snapback-persona: alice' -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"args":{}}'
curl -s -X POST $U/m/add -H 'x-snapback-persona: alice' -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"args":{"body":"hi"}}'
```

`snapback4 dev` serves on that address (choose another with `--port N`, or
`--port 0` for a free one; the first stdout line is the URL). A taken port
refuses the start at once with `E_ADDRINUSE`, naming the holder's pid. The JS client (`snapback4/client`) stays the
paved surface for apps: it adds sync, offline writes and live queries.

## Identity

In development, `x-snapback-persona: alice` acts as `dev:alice`: same machine
only, `[A-Za-z0-9_-]+`, and only for a name `use personas` declares; any other
name is 401 E_AUTH naming the declaration (a schema without `use personas`
admits any name). `snapback4 serve` refuses personas. Everywhere else send `Authorization: Bearer <token>`, from
`/auth/guest` (`{}`), `/auth/signup` or `/auth/login` (`{"email","password"}`),
each answering `{"session":{principal,kind,token,expiresAt}}`; a worker's token
comes from `snapback4 token --service <name>`. Send one credential, not both.

## Wire and client envelopes

| Call | Success | Refusal / pending |
|---|---|---|
| `POST /m/<op>` | `{state:"sent", id?, seq, result, generation, store_id, head}`; inserted row: `result.id` | `{state:"failed", id?, why, current?, store_id, head}`; decode/auth/unknown-op failures can be `{denied}` |
| `POST /q/<op>` | `{data, complete, cut, next, seq, tables, viewer, generation, store_id}`; list, row, shape or scalar | `{denied, complete:false, …}` or `{loading:true,data:null,complete:false,…}`; loading data is a placeholder, never an empty answer |
| `POST /assets` raw bytes | `{asset:{id,type,bytes,width?,height?,duration?,name?}}`; `?as=file&name=…` for files | `{denied}`; pass `{id:asset.id}` to the mutation |
| `await client.query(api.op, args)` | `Read<T>`: `{data, existence, complete, …}`; narrow with `isReady` | `{denied}`; one-shot reads settle or refuse; observers can also load |
| `await client.mutate(api.op, args)` | `Write<T>`: `{state:"sent", id, seq, result?, …}` | `{state:"failed", id, why, …}` or `{state:"pending", id, …}`; pending is not success |

SDK envelopes differ from raw HTTP. Check `loading` and `denied` before `data`; HTTP status alone is insufficient.
An omitted mutation request id is omitted from its reply, never the inserted
row id. Send an id once per intended write to retry safely; SDK clients mint
it. Replays omit historical result/current; see the Operations contract below.

## Operations

- `/q/<op>` with `{"args":{..}}` answers `{data, complete, cut, next, seq, tables, viewer}`
  or `{denied}`. `data` is what the query returns, as is: a list, a row, a shape,
  or a scalar (a keyed view read `totals[owner]` answers the number itself).
  Page by passing `next` back as the query's cursor argument.
- `/m/<op>` with `{"id"?, "args":{..}}` answers `{state:"sent", id, seq, result}` or
  `{state:"failed", id, why, current?}`, both HTTP 200 (see Refusals for the
  status codes). `result` is what the mutation returns (an inserted row's id is
  `result.id`); the top-level `id` is the write id you sent, omitted without one. With an `id` (a unique string the caller mints once per
  intended write), a retry of the same call applies at most once and answers
  `replayed: true`; the same `id` with another operation or other arguments is
  `{state:"failed", why:{code:"E_ID_REUSED"}}`. Without an `id`, every POST is a
  new write.
  A replay omits `result` and `current`: the durable receipt retains execution
  identity, not historical values or read authority. Read current data with a
  query instead of treating an absent replay result as a new-write result.
- Values: `time` is Unix milliseconds; `bytes` is lowercase hex; a reference is
  its row's id string. Numbers: see below.
- Uploads: `POST /assets` with the file's bytes as the raw body (not JSON, not multipart; the
  server sniffs JPEG, PNG, WebP or MP4 and ignores `Content-Type`), as the uploader, answers
  `{asset: {id, type, bytes, width, height, duration}}`, or `{denied}`: `E_ASSET_TYPE` (other
  bytes), `E_ASSET_TOO_LARGE`, 400 for an empty body, 401 `E_AUTH`, 413 past 64 MiB. Pass the
  asset to a mutation's `image`/`video`/`file` argument as `{"id": "<asset id>"}`; `GET /assets/<id>`
  returns the bytes to a viewer the row's read rule admits, else 404 `E_ASSET_UNKNOWN`.
  `curl -X POST $U/assets -H 'x-snapback-persona: alice' --data-binary @receipt.png`
- A job is not a client call (E_OP): a schedule fires it, and in dev
  `/dev/jobs/<job>` or `snapback4 run <job>` runs one.
- GET `/changes?since=<seq>&wait=25` long-polls. It answers `{seq, tables,
  generation, store_id}` when a commit lands or after `wait` seconds: re-query
  what reads those tables, then poll again from the new `seq`. `/sync` is the
  device replication protocol; use the client rather than reimplementing it.
- A held wait is bounded. A full host answers 503 `E_STREAMS` (Retry-After: 2);
  one principal past its share of held polls (one per tab or device, default
  16) gets 429 `E_STREAMS` (Retry-After: 5). Both are retryable and admitted
  nothing: wait for Retry-After, poll `/changes?since=<seq>` without `wait`
  meanwhile, then hold again. The JS client does this for you.
- A host that runs the device itself (the native module or the wasm device
  without `snapback4/client`) must route each device `query` reply: some say
  "ask the server" (`unknown` without `retained`) and carry a placeholder
  `data`. "The device reply" in CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md §7 is that contract.

## Numbers, money and decimals

Every number on the wire is a JSON integer within ±(2^53 − 1); there are no
floats. `money CUR` (scale 2 unless given) and `decimal S` hold an integer
scaled by 10^S and travel as that integer, in both directions:

```quarry
table expenses:
  owner: principal
  amount: money USD                 -- $12.50 travels as 1250
  rate: decimal 4                   -- 0.0725 travels as 725
  place: json <=400 ?               -- numbers inside are integers too
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
mutation addExpense(amount: money USD, rate: decimal 4, place: json <=400 ?):
  require amount > 0 else BAD_AMOUNT
  return insert expenses { owner: viewer, amount, rate, place }
query spent():
  return sum(expenses where .owner = viewer, .amount)
```

```sh
curl -s -X POST $U/m/addExpense -H 'x-snapback-persona: alice' -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"args":{"amount":1250,"rate":725,"place":{"latE6":37774900,"lngE6":-122419400}}}'
```

`spent` answers `{"data":1250,..}`: cents, not dollars. `12.5` and `1000.0`
refuse HTTP 400 E_INPUT ("argument amount: an integer"), `"12.50"` too ("expected
an integer amount in minor units"), and so does a fraction anywhere inside a
`json` value ("a number must be an integer in the signed 53-bit range"). Scale with rounding before sending:
`Math.round(dollars * 100)`, since `0.29 * 100` is `28.999999999999996`; in
Python send `round(x * 100)`, because `json.dumps(10.0)` writes `10.0`.
Format on display, `(cents / 100).toFixed(2)`. Keep coordinates and
measurements as scaled integers (microdegrees, milligrams) or as text.

## Development doors

Dev only, same machine; `serve` refuses each one.

- `/seed` with a JSON array `[{"table":"t","row":{"id":"..",..}}, ..]` writes
  every row as the system in one commit, rules bypassed (types, constraints,
  caps and sync still apply), and answers `{seeded: N}`; anything else is 400
  E_INPUT, and a row the engine refuses answers 200 `{denied}` with nothing
  written. Rows are written like inserts: omitted columns take their
  `default` (`now` is the dev clock), sequence columns are assigned and must be
  omitted (E_SEQUENCE_SUPPLIED), and the `$`-prefixed fields `snapback4 data`
  prints are recorded by the engine itself: drop them before seeding. It is an upsert by `id`: every row
  names its own (any unique string; `idFrom(label)` gives a stable one), a
  reseed updates the mutable columns of an existing row, and a different value
  for an `immutable` column refuses E_CONSTRAINT ("… is immutable; /seed is an
  upsert by id …"): keep the original value, use a new id, `POST /dev/reset`
  first, or restart dev with `--memory`. A principal field holds `"dev:alice"`,
  not `"alice"`.
- `/dev/reset` empties the store for the next test, in one transaction: every
  application row, view and maintained column, the sync log, the effect, job
  and action queues, uploaded assets, and accounts with their sessions
  (signed-up users, guests). The adopted schema, personas and service seats
  (`snapback4 token`, the dev worker's) stay. The store takes a new identity:
  a local client re-acquires the new store by itself, an online client halts
  with `E_STORE_REPLACED` (`createApp` reopens it). The head keeps counting.
  The body is empty, a `/seed` array, or `{"seed": [..], "clock": "reset"}`
  (either key optional): seed rows are written as `/seed` writes them, in the
  same transaction (a refused row resets nothing), and `"clock": "reset"`
  drops `--clock-offset` and every advance (by default the dev clock stays).
  It answers `{reset: true, seeded: N, generation, storeId, previousStoreId,
  head, now, clockOffsetMs, ms}` in a few milliseconds on a small store;
  `snapback4 reset [--port N] [--seed fixtures] [--clock reset]` posts it.
- `/dev/clock/advance` with `{"duration":"+2m"}` moves the dev clock, sweeps
  expiry, fires due schedules and runs queued jobs (`snapback4 clock advance 2m`).
- `/dev/jobs/<job>` with `{"args":{..}}` runs one job now and answers its run.
- GET `/dev/status` (no credential) is the readiness probe: it names the
  server's `pid`, `project`, whether the store is `temporary`, the served
  generation, the dev clock and the declared personas.
- `snapback4 run <op> --as bob` beside a running dev forwards to these routes.

## Refusals

| HTTP | Meaning / body |
|---|---|
| 200 | Operation success, or decoded application refusal (`denied` / `state:"failed", why`); inspect the body |
| 202 | An admitted asynchronous action is still running |
| 400 | Malformed JSON/wire arguments or wrong operation door, `denied` |
| 401 | Missing/dead credential, production persona, failed login or disabled identity method, `denied` E_AUTH |
| 403 | Development door on serve, read-only token attempting a write, or supervised-worker refresh, `denied` |
| 404 | Unknown route/operation, or hidden/missing asset, `denied` |
| 413 | Request exceeds the body ceiling, `denied` |
| 429 | Identity rate limit or per-principal wait capacity; retry as directed |
| 500 | Internal store/response-metadata failure, `denied` |
| 503 | Retryable refusal, store/infrastructure failure or host capacity; retry as directed |

Authentication refuses before an operation executes. Identity helpers in the
JS client preserve the `denied` body on 401/403; do not replace it with a proxy
error page. Rule/constraint refusals after operation admission remain HTTP 200.
Identity scope/value validation can also return 200 denied/E_INPUT; inspect the body.
After a streaming heartbeat starts, HTTP stays 200; inspect the final JSON for
`denied` if the session expires while waiting.

Two layers answer differently. The HTTP layer decodes the request against the
operation's signature before anything runs: a body that is not JSON, an
argument of the wrong JSON type or shape (a fraction, a string for an `int`,
a number for `text`), or an argument the operation does not take is **400**
`{denied:{code:"E_INPUT"}}`. No or a dead credential (or an undeclared
persona) is **401** E_AUTH; an unknown operation or route is **404** E_OP, and
a declared one at the wrong door (a job or query at `/m/`, a mutation at
`/q/`) is **400** E_OP naming the right one; a body past its ceiling (1 MiB of JSON, 16 MiB for `/seed` and `/dev/reset`) is **413**. Once
decoded, the operation runs, and everything it refuses is **200**: a query
answers `{denied}`, a mutation `{state:"failed", why}`. That includes value
checks made while it runs — a missing required argument, text outside its
bounds, `''` for a `text 1..500` argument too (`why.code` E_INPUT) — rules (E_RULE), constraints
(E_CONSTRAINT), a delete that rows still reference (E_REFERENCED, naming the
referencing column), `NOT_FOUND`, the program's own `require … else CODE` (with
`why.site` and `why.rule`), and a reused write id (E_ID_REUSED). A retryable
refusal is 503 instead. So branch on the body, not only the status; for an
`add(body: text <=200)` mutation:

```sh
curl -s -w ' %{http_code}\n' -X POST $U/m/add -H 'x-snapback-persona: alice' -d '{"args":{"body":12.5}}'
# {"denied":{"code":"E_INPUT","message":"argument body: expected text …"}} 400
curl -s -w ' %{http_code}\n' -X POST $U/m/add -H 'x-snapback-persona: alice' -d '{"args":{"body":"'"$(printf 'x%.0s' {1..300})"'"}}'
# {"state":"failed","why":{"code":"E_INPUT","message":"E_INPUT: body is longer than 200 characters"}} 200
```

A refusal is `{code, family, message, site?, rule?, rewrite?, guide?}`, and
`snapback4 why <code>` explains one. `E_WRITE_ID_REUSE` is the JS client's own
local refusal for the same mistake, before anything is sent; the wire's code is
`E_ID_REUSED`.

## Uploads and downloads

`POST /assets` takes the raw bytes as the body and answers `{"asset":{id,type,bytes,…}}`
or `{denied}`; a mutation then places `{"id": …}` in its `image`, `video` or `file`
argument. An image or video is sniffed from its bytes. A document for a `file`
column adds `?as=file&name=<percent-encoded filename>` and declares its type,
which the server checks against the bytes (`guide assets`, "Files"):

```sh
curl -s -X POST "$U/assets?as=file&name=March%20receipt.pdf" -H 'x-snapback-persona: alice' -H 'Content-Type: application/pdf' --data-binary @receipt.pdf
# {"asset":{"id":"…","type":"application/pdf","bytes":48213,"name":"March receipt.pdf","width":null,"height":null,"duration":null}}
curl -s -OJ "$U/assets/<id>" -H 'x-snapback-persona: alice'   # Content-Disposition: attachment names the file
```

`GET /assets/<id>` is 404 E_ASSET_UNKNOWN when hidden or gone; a file always
arrives as an attachment, and `Range` works on ordinary assets.

The full reference, including the rest of `/auth`, worker and action
doors, and clock headers, is "The wire" in CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md §5, which
ships in the package beside README.md.

//! `test.cases.jsonl` beside the fixture tables: the application's own
//! operations, in order, as named viewers, each with the outcome it expects.
//! One line is `{"as": "alice", "op": "addCard", "args": {..}, "expect": "sent"}`;
//! `expect` is `sent` (a mutation's default), `complete`, `capped`, `ok` (any
//! answer), or a refusal code (`E_RULE`, `NOT_FOUND`, a program's own `require`
//! code). A query's default is its `check` verdict: `complete`, or either
//! answer when `check` notes the query is never complete (N_NEVER_COMPLETE).
//! Optional `data` must equal the answer; `count` is the answer's length; `save`
//! names the answer so a later string argument `"$name"` or `"$name.field"`
//! reuses it. `as` is a declared persona, as on dev: an undeclared one is
//! refused E_AUTH.
use crate::project::persona_principal;
use snapback4_core::engine::refusal_trace::RefusalTrace;
use snapback4_core::ir::ProgramKind;
use snapback4_core::{Context, Engine, Refusal, WriteState, wire};
use snapback4_lang::Bound;
use serde_json::{Value as Json, json};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;
use std::path::Path;

pub const FILE: &str = "test.cases.jsonl";

/// Dev's cast check, for `test` and a local `run`: with `use personas`
/// declared, a bare name (or `dev:<name>`) outside the cast is refused as dev
/// refuses its header (E_AUTH); a principal id such as `user:…` passes as itself.
pub fn undeclared_persona(name: &str, declared: &[String]) -> Option<String> {
    if declared.is_empty() { return None; }
    let bare = match name.split_once(':') { None => name, Some(("dev", bare)) => bare, Some(_) => return None };
    (!declared.iter().any(|p| p == bare)).then(|| format!(
        "persona {bare} is not declared; `use personas {}` is this project's cast, and dev refuses {bare} with E_AUTH (401). Add {bare} to it, or use a declared persona (an outsider is a declared persona with no rows)",
        declared.join(", ")))
}

/// The persona a case without `as` runs as: alice when she is in the cast (or
/// there is none), else the first declared persona.
pub fn default_persona(declared: &[String]) -> &str {
    if declared.is_empty() || declared.iter().any(|p| p == "alice") { "alice" } else { &declared[0] }
}

/// One case as `test`'s query sweep reads it: which query, as whom, with
/// which arguments (`None` when they name a saved answer, which only the case
/// run has), expecting what.
pub struct Explicit { pub at: String, pub op: String, pub principal: String, pub args: Option<Json>, pub expect: Option<String> }

/// The cases file read leniently, before the sweep: a line that does not
/// parse is left to [`run`], which refuses it with its line number.
// @ref LLP 3000.036#15.2 — wave 7: the sweep runs a persona's own case arguments and labels a refusal its case expects.
pub fn explicit(fixture: &str, declared: &[String]) -> Vec<Explicit> {
    let Ok(text) = std::fs::read_to_string(Path::new(fixture).join(FILE)) else { return Vec::new() };
    fn saved(value: &Json) -> bool {
        match value { Json::String(text) => text.starts_with('$') && text.len() > 1, Json::Array(items) => items.iter().any(saved), Json::Object(fields) => fields.values().any(saved), _ => false }
    }
    text.lines().enumerate().filter_map(|(index, line)| {
        let line = line.trim();
        if line.is_empty() || line.starts_with("//") { return None; }
        let case: Json = serde_json::from_str(line).ok()?;
        let op = case.get("op")?.as_str()?.to_owned();
        let persona = case.get("as").and_then(Json::as_str).unwrap_or_else(|| default_persona(declared));
        let principal = if persona.contains(':') { persona.to_owned() } else { persona_principal(persona) };
        let args = Some(case.get("args").cloned().unwrap_or_else(|| json!({}))).filter(|args| args.is_object() && !saved(args));
        Some(Explicit { at: format!("{FILE}:{}", index + 1), op, principal, args, expect: case.get("expect").and_then(Json::as_str).map(str::to_owned) })
    }).collect()
}

/// Runs every case; prints one line each and returns (passed, failed).
/// `sources` are the compiled files, to quote a refusing clause's line.
pub fn run(engine: &mut Engine, bound: &Bound, fixture: &str, sources: &[(String, String)]) -> Result<(usize, usize), String> {
    let path = Path::new(fixture).join(FILE);
    let text = match std::fs::read_to_string(&path) {
        Ok(text) => text,
        Err(error) if error.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => return Ok((0, 0)),
        Err(error) => return Err(format!("cannot read {}: {error}", path.display())),
    };
    let mut saved = BTreeMap::<String, Json>::new();
    let (mut passed, mut failed) = (0, 0);
    let mut verdicts_shown = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
    let lines: Vec<(usize, &str)> = text.lines().enumerate().filter(|(_, line)| !line.trim().is_empty() && !line.trim_start().starts_with("//")).collect();
    // Every line is checked before any runs: a misspelled key must not pass
    // silently (wave 5: `"rows":0` passed against one row), and a typo on the
    // last line should not wait for every earlier case.
    let mut cases = Vec::with_capacity(lines.len());
    for (index, line) in &lines {
        let at = format!("{FILE}:{}", index + 1);
        let case: Json = serde_json::from_str(line).map_err(|error| format!("{at}: {error}"))?;
        validate(&case).map_err(|why| format!("{at}: {why}"))?;
        cases.push((at, case));
    }
    println!("test: cases from {FILE}");
    for (at, case) in cases {
        let Some(op) = case.get("op").and_then(Json::as_str) else { return Err(format!("{at}: each case names \"op\" (a query or mutation)")) };
        let Some(bound_program) = bound.programs.iter().find(|p| p.program.name == op) else {
            let starter = super::init::starter_fixture(FILE).is_some_and(|starter| starter == text);
            let hint = if starter { format!("\n  hint: {FILE} is the todo starter's unmodified cases for list/add/toggle/remove; `snapback4 init --adapt` removes it (write your own cases, `snapback4 guide testing`)") } else { String::new() };
            return Err(format!("{at}: unknown operation {op}{hint}"));
        };
        let program = bound_program.program.clone();
        let persona = case.get("as").and_then(Json::as_str).unwrap_or_else(|| default_persona(&bound.personas));
        let principal = if persona.contains(':') { persona.to_owned() } else { persona_principal(persona) };
        // A query's default expectation is check's verdict: a noted never-complete page passes capped.
        let never_complete = (program.kind == ProgramKind::Query).then(|| never_complete(bound, bound_program)).flatten();
        let explicit = case.get("expect").and_then(Json::as_str);
        let expect = explicit.unwrap_or(match program.kind { ProgramKind::Query if never_complete.is_some() => "ok", ProgramKind::Query => "complete", _ => "sent" });
        let label = case.get("label").and_then(Json::as_str).map(|l| format!(" — {l}")).unwrap_or_default();
        let raw_args = substitute(case.get("args").cloned().unwrap_or_else(|| json!({})), &saved).map_err(|why| format!("{at}: {why}"))?;
        let mut args = BTreeMap::new();
        if let Json::Object(fields) = &raw_args {
            for (name, value) in fields {
                let Some(kind) = program.args.get(name) else { return Err(format!("{at}: {op} takes no argument {name}")) };
                args.insert(name.clone(), wire::from_json(kind, value).map_err(|why| format!("{at}: argument {name}: {why}"))?);
            }
        } else { return Err(format!("{at}: \"args\" is an object")); }
        let trace = RefusalTrace::default();
        let context = Context::new(principal, args).with_refusal_trace(trace.clone());
        // (outcome word, answer) or a refusal
        let mut unavailable = false;
        let got: Result<(&str, Json), Refusal> = if let Some(why) = undeclared_persona(persona, &bound.personas) {
            Err(Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", why))
        } else { match program.kind {
            ProgramKind::Query => match engine.query(op, context) {
                Ok(outcome) => { unavailable = outcome.unavailable; Ok((if outcome.complete { "complete" } else { "capped" }, wire::to_json(&outcome.data))) },
                Err(refusal) => Err(refusal),
            },
            ProgramKind::Job => return Err(format!("{at}: {op} is a job; cases run queries and mutations")),
            _ => match engine.mutate(op, None, context) {
                Ok(outcome) => match outcome.state {
                    WriteState::Sent { result, .. } => Ok(("sent", wire::to_json(&result))),
                    WriteState::Failed { why, .. } => Err(why),
                },
                Err(error) => Err(error.refusal()),
            },
        } };
        let mut problems = Vec::new();
        let mut notes = Vec::new();
        let shown = match &got {
            Ok((word, answer)) => {
                let admitted = *word == expect || expect == "ok" || (expect == "complete" && *word == "sent");
                if !admitted { problems.push(if expect == "capped" && *word == "complete" {
                    "now complete — update the case: replace expect: \"capped\" with expect: \"complete\" (or omit expect); readable pinned-parent guards can now certify this page".to_owned()
                } else { format!("expected {expect}, got {word}") }); }
                if *word == "capped" && let Some(note) = &never_complete {
                    if admitted && explicit.is_none() && verdicts_shown.insert(op.to_owned()) { notes.push(format!("capped is this query's check verdict: {note}")); }
                    if !admitted { notes.push(format!("hint: {note}; a capped answer still holds every row you may read: add \"expect\":\"capped\" (or drop \"expect\"), or pin the page as the note's rewrite says (snapback4 check --verbose)")); }
                }
                if let Some(data) = case.get("data") && data != answer {
                    problems.push(format!("data differs: got {answer}"));
                    // @ref LLP 3000.036#15.12 — an unavailable answer is not a null value: say why it has none.
                    if unavailable { notes.push("null here is no answer (a client shows `loading`), not a null value: a ceiling cut it, or an `exists` over rows this viewer only partly reads found no row it may read, which cannot prove absence (snapback4 check --verbose)".into()); }
                }
                if let Some(count) = case.get("count").and_then(Json::as_u64) {
                    let length = answer.as_array().map(|a| a.len()).or_else(|| answer.get("items").and_then(Json::as_array).map(|a| a.len()));
                    if length != Some(count as usize) { problems.push(format!("expected {count} rows, got {}", length.map(|n| n.to_string()).unwrap_or_else(|| "a non-list".into()))); }
                }
                if let Some(name) = case.get("save").and_then(Json::as_str) { saved.insert(name.to_owned(), answer.clone()); }
                (*word).to_owned()
            }
            Err(refusal) => {
                let code = &refusal.code;
                if code != expect && !(expect == "denied" || expect == "refused" || expect == "failed") {
                    problems.push(format!("expected {expect}, got {code}: {}", refusal.message));
                    notes.extend(refusal_evidence(&trace, refusal, bound, sources, explicit));
                }
                code.clone()
            }
        };
        if problems.is_empty() {
            passed += 1;
            println!("      ok   {at} {op} ({persona}) → {shown}{label}");
        } else {
            failed += 1;
            println!("      FAIL {at} {op} ({persona}) → {shown}{label}: {}", problems.join("; "));

//! Expanded helpers can write from any expression block.
// @ref LLP 3000.022#14.3 — legacy eligibility examines the complete expanded write footprint.
use super::*;
pub(super) fn stmts_write_tables(stmts: &[Stmt], out: &mut Vec<(String, &'static str)>) {
    for stmt in stmts {
        match stmt {
            Stmt::Insert { table, .. } | Stmt::InsertIfAbsent { table, .. } => out.push((table.clone(), "insert")),
            Stmt::Update { table, .. } => out.push((table.clone(), "update")),
            Stmt::Upsert { table, .. } => out.push((table.clone(), "upsert")),
            Stmt::WriteTarget { target, upsert, .. } => out.push((target.table.clone(), if *upsert { "upsert" } else { "update" })),
            Stmt::Delete { table, .. } => out.push((table.clone(), "delete")),
            Stmt::DeleteTarget { target } => out.push((target.table.clone(), "delete")),
            _ => {}
        }
        match stmt {
            Stmt::Const { value, .. } | Stmt::Let { value, .. } | Stmt::Assign { value, .. } | Stmt::Push { value, .. } | Stmt::Return(value) => expr_write_tables(value, out),
            Stmt::Emit { payload: value, once, .. } => { expr_write_tables(value, out); if let Some(value) = once { expr_write_tables(value, out); } },
            Stmt::If { condition, then_body, else_body } => {
                expr_write_tables(condition, out);
                stmts_write_tables(then_body, out);
                stmts_write_tables(else_body, out);
            }
            Stmt::For { domain, body, .. } => {
                match domain {
                    LoopDomain::Page(scan) => scan_write_tables(scan, out),
                    LoopDomain::ExpressionRange { start, end_exclusive } => { expr_write_tables(start, out); expr_write_tables(end_exclusive, out); }
                    LoopDomain::DerivedArray { value, .. } => expr_write_tables(value, out),
                    LoopDomain::Recurse(recurse) => {
                        expr_write_tables(&recurse.seed, out);
                    }
                    LoopDomain::LiteralRange { .. } | LoopDomain::ArgList { .. } => {}
                }
                stmts_write_tables(body, out);
            }
            Stmt::Require { condition, .. } => expr_write_tables(condition, out),
            Stmt::Check { condition, current } => {
                expr_write_tables(condition, out);
                for key in &current.key { expr_write_tables(key, out); }
            }
            Stmt::InsertIfAbsent { row, .. } | Stmt::Insert { row, .. } => expr_write_tables(row, out),
            Stmt::Update { key, patch, .. } => {
                expr_write_tables(key, out);
                expr_write_tables(patch, out);
            }
            Stmt::Upsert { key, row, .. } => {
                expr_write_tables(key, out);
                expr_write_tables(row, out);
            }
            Stmt::WriteTarget { target, patch, condition, .. } => {
                for key in &target.key { expr_write_tables(key, out); }
                expr_write_tables(patch, out);
                if let Some(condition) = condition { stmts_write_tables(&condition.setup, out); expr_write_tables(&condition.value, out); }
            }
            Stmt::Delete { key, .. } => expr_write_tables(key, out),
            Stmt::DeleteTarget { target } => {
                for key in &target.key { expr_write_tables(key, out); }
            }
            Stmt::Break | Stmt::Continue => {}
        }
    }
}

fn scan_write_tables(scan: &Scan, out: &mut Vec<(String, &'static str)>) {
    if let Some(filter) = &scan.filter { expr_write_tables(filter, out); }
    if let Some(range) = &scan.range {
        for bound in [&range.gt, &range.gte, &range.lt, &range.lte].into_iter().flatten() { expr_write_tables(bound, out); }
    }
    for key in &scan.prefix {
        match key { IndexKeyExpr::Scalar(expr) | IndexKeyExpr::WordPrefix(expr) => expr_write_tables(expr, out) }
    }
    for cursor in [&scan.after, &scan.before].into_iter().flatten() { expr_write_tables(cursor, out); }
}

    match expr {

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum RuleTerm {
    /// One authorized point read through a row-local typed reference.
    Reference { table: String, column: String, field: String, old: bool },
    /// Bounded live reads; a distinct variant makes old decoders refuse.
    ReferenceChain { column: String, hops: Vec<RuleHop>, old: bool },
    Field(String),
    OldField(String),
    NextField(String),
    Viewer,
    Literal(Value),
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct RuleHop { pub table: String, pub field: String }

pub const MAX_RULE_HOPS: usize = 3;

impl RuleTerm {
    pub fn reference_source(&self) -> Option<(&str, bool)> {
        match self {
            Self::Reference { column, old, .. } | Self::ReferenceChain { column, old, .. } => Some((column, *old)),
            _ => None,
        }
    }
    pub fn references(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = (&str, &str)> {
        let (one, many) = match self {
            Self::Reference { table, field, .. } => (Some((table.as_str(), field.as_str())), &[][..]),
            Self::ReferenceChain { hops, .. } => (None, hops.as_slice()),
            _ => (None, &[][..]),
        };
        one.into_iter().chain(many.iter().map(|hop| (hop.table.as_str(), hop.field.as_str())))
    }
}

# Deploy: upgrade, real accounts and schema evolution

Deploy into the running serve process without restarting it. A release is
immutable source over one durable store; production admits bearer tokens.

```sh
bunx snapback4 check
bunx snapback4 test
bunx snapback4 deploy ./deployment
bunx snapback4 serve ./deployment --port 4400
```

See [SERVER.md — Release lifecycle](SERVER.md#release-lifecycle).

## Upgrade

`snapback4 upgrade [version]` resolves once and exactly pins core and installed
Expo adapters with npm/bun/pnpm. Rebuild native apps after adapter changes;
commit the lockfile, run check/typecheck/acceptance. `test` uses ./fixtures;
`--fixture dir` overrides it. REL owns the persona-to-password checklist.

## Deploy and password identity

Add `use identity password` to your schema (the recipe below includes it).
Deploy from the app directory; keep the deployment directory on durable storage:

`serve --port N` accepts 0 for an allocated port or an inclusive range A-B, choosing its first free port. Invalid input refuses E_INPUT. Stdout's first line is the actual URL; stderr includes `SNAPBACK4_READY verb=serve … url=…`. Wait for readiness before requests. For a redeploy, run deploy on the same host while serve stays running, then verify a read and write with a real account. Its private owner socket serializes activation; failed validation keeps the current release. A dev or older unreachable owner requires stopping that owned process before retrying. See SERVER.md#release-lifecycle.

Layout: `releases/N/` holds each backend; `store.sqlite` holds rows, sessions,
receipts and queues; `assets/` holds uploads; `releases.jsonl` records activation.
`current` is a **text file containing a release number**, not a symlink. The
database's release metadata is authoritative; the file is a convenience copy.
Back up the whole directory with the server and external workers stopped;
include SQLite sidecars, assets and releases. See CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md §6
for the tested stopped-backup/restore procedure and external-effect caveats.

```sh
U=http://127.0.0.1:4400
curl -s "$U/auth/signup" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"email":"admin@example.test","password":"replace-with-a-private-password"}'
# {session:{principal,kind,token,expiresAt,scope}}
curl -s "$U/auth/login" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"email":"admin@example.test","password":"replace-with-a-private-password"}'
```

For an executable headless account-seeding recipe, open `guide auth` or `guide headless`.
Use `signup`/`login` from `snapback4/auth` in the app; keep the returned
session in its session store and send `Authorization: Bearer <token>`.
Read the `session` or `denied` body (guide wire): wrong credentials and a
disabled identity method are 401 E_AUTH. A production persona is 401;
`/seed` and other development doors are 403. Signup creates an account,
not an admin. Sessions survive ordinary restarts and deployments of the same
store until expiry or revocation. A reset/replaced store requires sign-in again;
never deploy by deleting the store to make adoption pass.

## First admin: a service seat

Grant the signed-up account's principal through an operator seat. This recipe
permits ops and existing admins to insert grants, with no public first-user race:

```quarry
use identity password
table admins:
  principal: principal
  unique principal
  read <- .principal = viewer or viewer = 'service:ops'
  insert <- viewer = 'service:ops' or exists admins[principal = viewer]
mutation grantAdmin(principal: principal):
  return insert admins { principal }
table notes:
  owner: principal
  body: text <=200
  archived: bool default false
  status: enum('draft', 'live') default 'draft'
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
```

```sh
TOKEN=$(bunx snapback4 token --service ops --release ./deployment)
`snapback4 sql 'SELECT id, archived FROM notes' --release ./deployment --json`
returns positional rows under `columns`; `--json-objects` returns object rows
with direct boolean columns as true/false. Schema-qualified relations, recursive
or shadowing/forward-reference CTEs need positional --json. Inspection bypasses read rules and can
show expired stored rows; it is an operator tool, not an app query surface.
Bind loopback behind a TLS proxy. Forward Authorization and preserve response
bodies; allow long polls without buffering or early timeouts. Non-loopback
HTTP requires `--i-understand-cleartext` and a trusted private hop behind TLS.
Identity rate keys use the socket peer, not forwarded headers. Serve refuses
personas and dev doors even through a local proxy; see SERVER.md for capacity.

//! Rule evaluation and the static facts about it that the engine hoists: a
//! rule that is proven true by the scanned prefix (`decided_true`) costs no
//! probes; a rule whose deciding fields are pinned by the prefix (`uniform`) is
//! decided once for the whole range. Copied from Snapback 2's uniformity
//! analysis (LLP 2000.000 §5.1); the certificate that priced these is gone,
//! the analysis is not.

use crate::ir::{Expr, IndexKeyExpr, Input, Merge, Scan};
use crate::schema::{ColumnType, RuleExpr, RuleTerm, Schema, Table};
use crate::value::Value;
use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Visibility {
    /// One verdict serves every row of the scanned prefix.
    pub uniform: bool,
    /// That verdict is `true` before any row is read.
    pub decided_true: bool,
}

pub fn scan_visibility(schema: &Schema, scan: &Scan) -> Visibility {
    let Some(table) = schema.table(&scan.table) else { return Visibility::default() };
    let Some(index) = table.index(&scan.index) else { return Visibility::default() };
    let pinned = |name: &str| index.components.iter().zip(&scan.prefix).find_map(|(part, key)| (part.column().is_some_and(|column| column == name)).then(|| key.scalar()).flatten());
    visibility(&scan.table, table, &table.rules.read, &pinned)

}

// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.5 — a missing upsert may insert only when the complete held group and its key decide that no hidden target can exist.
pub fn upsert_absence(schema: &Schema, name: &str, index: &str, key: &[Expr]) -> Result<Visibility, &'static str> {
    let table = schema.table(name).ok_or("the target is online only")?;
    let sync = table.sync.as_ref().ok_or("the target is online only")?;
    if sync.horizon.is_some() { return Err("the target has a horizon"); }
    let rule = table.rules.read.device_audience_rule();
    // Public subset sync has no whole-table absence proof. Its group coverage
    // is a separate decision; do not infer it merely from the public read rule.
    if rule.is_uniformly_readable() && sync.audience != crate::schema::Audience::Public { return Err("the key's synced group is not established"); }
    let index = table.index(index).ok_or("the key does not pin readability")?;
    let pinned = crate::small_map::collect(index.components.iter().zip(key).filter_map(|(part, expression)| part.column().map(|column| (column, expression))));
    let visibility = prefix_visibility(name, table, &rule, &pinned);
    if !visibility.uniform { return Err("the key does not pin readability"); }
    Ok(visibility)
}

// @ref LLP 3000.007#18.1 — simplify self-witnesses, then pre-check only the leading conjuncts whose fields the scope pins, in evaluation order.
pub(super) fn prefix_decision(schema: &Schema, scan: &Scan) -> Option<RuleExpr> {
    let table = schema.table(&scan.table)?;
    let index = table.index(&scan.index)?;
    let pinned: BTreeMap<_,_> = crate::small_map::collect(index.components.iter().zip(&scan.prefix).filter_map(|(part, key)| part.column().zip(key.scalar())));
    if table.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| pinned.contains_key(&t.reference)) { return Some(table.rules.read.clone()); }
    let rule = simplify_prefix(&table.rules.read, &scan.table, table, &pinned);
    if matches!(rule, RuleExpr::Allow) { return None; }
    let mut terms = Vec::new();
    conjuncts(rule, &mut terms);
    let decided: Vec<_> = terms.into_iter().take_while(|term| {
        let mut fields = BTreeSet::new();
        term.fields(&mut fields);
        fields.iter().all(|field| pinned.contains_key(field))
    }).collect();
    (!decided.is_empty()).then_some(RuleExpr::And(decided))
}

// Flatten only conjunctions, preserving left-to-right short-circuit order.
fn conjuncts(rule: RuleExpr, out: &mut Vec<RuleExpr>) {
    match rule { RuleExpr::And(terms) => for term in terms { conjuncts(term, out); }, rule => out.push(rule) }
}

fn simplify_prefix(rule: &RuleExpr, name: &str, table: &Table, pinned: &BTreeMap<&String, &Expr>) -> RuleExpr {
    if rule_true(rule, name, table, &|name| pinned.iter().find_map(|(key, value)| (key.as_str() == name).then_some(*value))) { return RuleExpr::Allow; }
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::And(terms) => {
            let mut out = Vec::new();
            for term in terms { conjuncts(simplify_prefix(term, name, table, pinned), &mut out); }
            out.retain(|term| !matches!(term, RuleExpr::Allow));
            RuleExpr::And(out)
        },
        RuleExpr::Or(_) => {
            // Use the same self-witness absorption as uniformity, but remove
            // the absorbed field read from the expression we actually run.
            let terms = rule.disjuncts();
            RuleExpr::Or(terms.iter().filter(|term| !terms.iter().any(|other| self_probe_absorbs(name, table, term, other))).map(|term| simplify_prefix(term, name, table, pinned)).collect())
        }
        RuleExpr::Not(inner) => RuleExpr::Not(Box::new(simplify_prefix(inner, name, table, pinned))),
        _ => rule.clone(),
    }
}

/// A read rule `A or G` whose disjunct `G` reads no column of the row (a
/// role such as `exists staff[viewer]`, or a service seat) is true for every
/// row whenever `G` holds for this caller. The page is then decided once, per
/// caller: a staff console's list is complete for staff, and is the same
/// per-row walk as before for everyone else.
///
/// Only grants whose every fact the caller could read are used, so `complete`
/// discloses nothing beyond the caller's own readable rows: each probe's
/// target row must be readable by its own read rule once the probe key pins
/// it (`.principal = viewer`, `public`, or the same probe), and a negated
/// probe is never a grant.
// @ref LLP 3000.036#12 — the role-guard completeness proof deferred in §11, server-side and per caller.
pub fn grant_decision(schema: &Schema, scan: &Scan) -> Option<RuleExpr> {
    guard_decision(schema, scan, false)
}

/// The disjuncts of `scan`'s read rule that decide the page for a caller they
/// admit: the readable grants (`grant_decision`), and each disjunct whose every
/// row column the scan's prefix pins and whose probed rows its holders can
/// read (`pinned_probe_readable`), such as `exists boards[.board, owner =
/// viewer]` under `.board = b`. The page walk (`read.rs`) evaluates them once,
/// with the prefix's values for the pinned columns: when one holds, every row
/// the scan can visit is readable, so the page is decided as a uniform page
/// is. Each verdict reads only rows the caller may read, so `complete`
/// discloses nothing beyond them.
// @ref LLP 3000.036#16.3 — a parent probe the pins decide is a page guard, as a grant is.
pub fn page_guard_decision(schema: &Schema, scan: &Scan) -> Option<RuleExpr> {
    guard_decision(schema, scan, true)
}

    let pinned = if pinned_guards { scan_pinned(table, scan) } else { Vec::new() };
    let guards: Vec<RuleExpr> = rule.disjuncts().into_iter()
        .filter(|disjunct| {

    pub(super) fn insert_upsert(&mut self, table_name: &str, addressed_index: &str, row: Supplied) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {

            Err(RuntimeError::Input(_)) => Err(self.no_write_target()),
            result => result,
        }
    }

//! The default `init --web` and `init --expo` starters: one table, one live
//! query, three mutations and one screen. Toolchain pins come from the
//! maintained reference apps, so the starters move with them; the reference
//! chat apps themselves stay reachable as `--template chat`.
use serde_json::{Value, json};

const SCHEMA: &str = include_str!("starter/schema.q");
/// Rows and cases for the starter's own `todos` schema; init writes them only
/// beside that schema (see `init`), never beside a project's own.
pub(super) const FIXTURES: [(&str, &str); 2] = [
    ("fixtures/todos.jsonl", include_str!("starter/fixtures/todos.jsonl")),
    ("fixtures/test.cases.jsonl", include_str!("starter/fixtures/test.cases.jsonl")),
];

/// What init knows about the project before it writes the starter.
pub(super) struct Shape {
    /// The page and app name, from the directory.
    pub title: String,
    pub password: bool,
    /// Personas of a kept schema that declares `use personas` and no
    /// `use identity`: nobody can sign in, so the screen picks a persona.
    pub personas: Option<Vec<String>>,
    /// The screen behind the sign-in: the todo list over the starter's own
    /// operations, or the neutral `Home` beside a schema without them.
    pub body: Body,
    /// What the kept schema syncs, for the README's offline sentence.
    pub offline: Offline,
}

/// Which tables of the project's schema a device holds offline: the README
/// and init's summary say what works offline from this, never from the
/// starter's own (synced) todos schema.
// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#7.6 — wave 7 (R3): "works offline" was printed beside online-only schemas.
#[derive(Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Debug)]
pub(super) enum Offline {
    /// The schema does not compile (yet), or there is none: no claim either way.
    Unknown,
    /// The tables that declare `sync`, and how many tables there are (views excluded).
    Tables { synced: Vec<String>, total: usize },
}

impl Offline {
    /// The README paragraph (it always starts with "Offline", which is how
    /// `init --adapt` finds it again), wrapped like the rest of the README.
    pub(super) fn paragraph(&self, platform: Platform) -> String {
        let lead = match platform { Platform::Web => "Offline:", Platform::Expo => "Offline, in device builds and on the web:" };
        let body = match self {
            Offline::Unknown => "tables that declare `sync` read and write offline and sync when the link returns; the others read from the server, and writes to them made offline are kept on this device and sent when the link returns (`npx snapback4 guide sync`).".to_owned(),
            Offline::Tables { synced, .. } if synced.is_empty() => format!("no table in `snapback/*.q` declares `sync`, so reads need the server. Writes made offline are kept {} and sent when the link returns. Add `sync` to a table to read it offline (`npx snapback4 guide sync`).", if platform == Platform::Web { "in this browser's principal journal" } else { "on this device" }),
            Offline::Tables { synced, total } if synced.len() >= *total => "every table declares `sync`, so the app reads and writes offline and syncs when the link returns.".to_owned(),
            Offline::Tables { synced, .. } => format!("the synced {} ({}) {} and {} offline and {} when the link returns; the other tables read from the server, and writes to them made offline are sent when the link returns (`npx snapback4 guide sync`).",
                if synced.len() == 1 { "table" } else { "tables" }, synced.iter().map(|name| format!("`{name}`")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", "),
                if synced.len() == 1 { "reads" } else { "read" }, if synced.len() == 1 { "writes" } else { "write" }, if synced.len() == 1 { "syncs" } else { "sync" }),
        };
        wrap(&format!("{lead} {body}"), 80)
    }

    /// init's one-line summary of the same fact, or nothing when unknown.
    pub(super) fn summary(&self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            Offline::Unknown => "",
            Offline::Tables { synced, .. } if synced.is_empty() => ", online reads (no table declares `sync`; offline writes queue in the write journal)",
            Offline::Tables { .. } => ", offline-capable for its synced tables",
        }
    }
}

/// Wrap at `width`, never inside a `code span`.
fn wrap(text: &str, width: usize) -> String {
    let mut words: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
    for word in text.split(' ') {
        match words.last_mut() {
            Some(open) if open.matches('`').count() % 2 == 1 => { open.push(' '); open.push_str(word); }
            _ => words.push(word.to_owned()),
        }
    }
    let mut lines: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
    for word in &words {
        match lines.last_mut() {
            Some(line) if line.len() + 1 + word.len() <= width => { line.push(' '); line.push_str(word); }
            _ => lines.push(word.clone()),
        }
    }
    lines.join("\n")
}

/// A README with its offline paragraph replaced by `OFFLINE`, and 0.4.22's
/// claim in the opening paragraph rewritten to today's opening, so `init
/// --adapt` recognizes an unmodified starter README whatever its schema
/// synced when it was written.
pub(super) fn without_offline(text: &str) -> String {
    let legacy = [
        ("the Snapback 4 web starter. It works offline and syncs when the link returns.\n", "the Snapback 4 web starter.\n\nOFFLINE\n"),
        ("started from the Snapback 4 Expo starter. Development and production builds\nopen the native device and work offline; Expo Go uses the online client; web\nuses the wasm device.\n",
            "started from the Snapback 4 Expo starter. Development and production builds\nopen the native device; Expo Go uses the online client; web uses the wasm\ndevice.\n\nOFFLINE\n"),
    ];
    let mut text = text.to_owned();
    for (old, new) in legacy { text = text.replacen(old, new, 1); }
    text.split("\n\n").map(|paragraph| if paragraph.starts_with("Offline") { "OFFLINE" } else { paragraph }).collect::<Vec<_>>().join("\n\n")
}

    }
}

pub fn pinned_literals(prefix: &[Value]) -> Vec<IndexKeyExpr> {
    prefix.iter().map(|value| IndexKeyExpr::Scalar(Expr::Literal(value.clone()))).collect()
}

/// Something that can answer an `exists`/`created` probe while a rule runs.
pub trait Probe {
    fn reference(&mut self, _table: &str, _id: &Value, _field: &str) -> Result<Option<Value>, crate::error::RuntimeError> { Ok(None) }
    /// Exact count-predicate transitions, distinct from ordinary witness expiry.
    fn take_changes(&mut self) -> Option<Vec<(i64, super::rules::Verdict)>> { None }
    /// Exchange the private expiry accumulator; ordinary probes have none.
    fn replace_expiry(&mut self, _expiry: Option<i64>) -> Option<i64> { None }
    /// Coverage of the most recent leaf, consumed once by the shared evaluator.
    fn take_unknown(&mut self) -> bool { false }
    /// Called only when evaluation loads a field, after short-circuit decisions.
    fn field(&mut self, _field: &str, _old: bool) {}
    /// Apply the schema's point-key null rule before storage access. Timeline
    /// evaluation can visit operands that logical short-circuiting would skip.
    fn exists(&mut self, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value], equalities: &[(String, Value)]) -> Result<bool, crate::error::RuntimeError>;
    fn created(&mut self, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value]) -> bool;
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Default)]
pub enum Verdict { Readable, Denied, #[default] Unknown }

impl Verdict {
    fn not(self) -> Self {
        match self { Self::Readable => Self::Denied, Self::Denied => Self::Readable, Self::Unknown => Self::Unknown }
    }
    fn combine(self, other: Self, conjunction: bool) -> Self {
        let decisive = if conjunction { Self::Denied } else { Self::Readable };
        if self == decisive || other == decisive { decisive }
        else if self == Self::Unknown || other == Self::Unknown { Self::Unknown }
        else { decisive.not() }
    }
}

/// Evaluate a rule over a row image (and its old image, for update rules).
pub fn evaluate(rule: &RuleExpr, viewer: &str, old: Option<&Value>, next: &Value, created_allowed: bool, probe: &mut dyn Probe) -> Result<bool, crate::error::RuntimeError> {
    evaluate_verdict(rule, viewer, old, next, created_allowed, probe).map(|verdict| verdict == Verdict::Readable)
}

pub(super) fn evaluate_verdict(rule: &RuleExpr, viewer: &str, old: Option<&Value>, next: &Value, created_allowed: bool, probe: &mut dyn Probe) -> Result<Verdict, crate::error::RuntimeError> {
    let prior = probe.replace_expiry(None);
    let result = timeline(rule, viewer, old, next, created_allowed, probe);
    probe.replace_expiry(match &result {
        Ok(answer) if answer.value == Verdict::Readable => earliest(prior, answer.changes.first().map(|(time, _)| *time)),
        _ => prior,
    });
    result.map(|answer| answer.value)
}

pub(super) fn earliest(a: Option<i64>, b: Option<i64>) -> Option<i64> {
    match (a, b) { (Some(a), Some(b)) => Some(a.min(b)), (a, b) => a.or(b) }
}

struct Timeline {
    value: Verdict,
    changes: Vec<(i64, Verdict)>,
    // Proven from rule structure and verdicts, never from witness deadlines.
    invariant: bool,
}
impl Timeline {
    fn constant(value: Verdict) -> Self { Self { value, changes: Vec::new(), invariant: true } }
    fn combine(self, other: Self, conjunction: bool) -> Self {
        let (mut left, mut right) = (self.value, other.value);
        let mut result = Self::constant(left.combine(right, conjunction));
        let decisive = if conjunction { Verdict::Denied } else { Verdict::Readable };
        result.invariant = (self.invariant && left == decisive)
            || (other.invariant && right == decisive) || (self.invariant && other.invariant);
        let (mut a, mut b) = (self.changes.as_slice(), other.changes.as_slice());
        let mut previous = result.value;
        while let Some(time) = earliest(a.first().map(|(t, _)| *t), b.first().map(|(t, _)| *t)) {
            // All rows expiring at the same instant disappear together. This
            // preserves correlations, including A or not A and A and not A.
            if let Some((head, tail)) = a.split_first() && head.0 == time { left = head.1; a = tail; }
            if let Some((head, tail)) = b.split_first() && head.0 == time { right = head.1; b = tail; }
            let value = left.combine(right, conjunction);
            if value != previous { result.changes.push((time, value)); previous = value; }
        }
        result
    }
}

// @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — next_expiry is the first expiry boundary that changes
// the complete bound rule's verdict, not a branch or first witness lifetime.
// Leaves prove their timeline within the original shared allowance. Combining
// those timelines performs no further storage reads.
fn timeline(rule: &RuleExpr, viewer: &str, old: Option<&Value>, next: &Value, created_allowed: bool, probe: &mut dyn Probe) -> Result<Timeline, crate::error::RuntimeError> {
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::And(terms) | RuleExpr::Or(terms) => {
            let conjunction = matches!(rule, RuleExpr::And(_));
            let decisive = if conjunction { Verdict::Denied } else { Verdict::Readable };
            let mut answer = Timeline::constant(decisive.not());
            for term in terms {
                answer = answer.combine(timeline(term, viewer, old, next, created_allowed, probe)?, conjunction);
                // Whole-rule expiry needs the remaining alternatives unless
                // structure/verdicts alone prove them irrelevant. An exists
                // with a permanent witness visits exactly the same branches
                // as one with a finite witness, under the same physical bound.
                // Logical guard narrowing does not assume this traversal skips
                // an operand: ordinary unique null points return a known miss
                // before touching storage (LLP 3000.013 §2.2).
                if answer.value == decisive && answer.invariant { break; }
            }
            Ok(answer)
        }
        RuleExpr::Not(inner) => {
            let mut answer = timeline(inner, viewer, old, next, created_allowed, probe)?;
            answer.value = answer.value.not();
            for (_, value) in &mut answer.changes { *value = value.not(); }
            Ok(answer)
        }
        _ => {
            probe.take_changes();
            let mut missing = false;
            let value = evaluate_leaf(rule, viewer, old, next, created_allowed, probe, &mut missing)?;
            let expiry = probe.replace_expiry(None);
            let mut answer = Timeline::constant(if probe.take_unknown() || missing { Verdict::Unknown } else if value { Verdict::Readable } else { Verdict::Denied });
            if let Some(changes) = probe.take_changes() {
                answer.changes = changes;
                answer.invariant = false;
                return Ok(answer);
            }
            // An absent witness stays absent as rows expire. Positive probes
            // (including created facts with extra tests) need their alternatives
            // in both lifetime worlds; the observed deadline cannot decide this.
            answer.invariant = rule_probes(rule) == 0 || answer.value == Verdict::Denied;
            if answer.value == Verdict::Readable && let Some(time) = expiry { answer.changes.push((time, Verdict::Denied)); }
            Ok(answer)
        }
    }
}

fn evaluate_leaf(rule: &RuleExpr, viewer: &str, old: Option<&Value>, next: &Value, created_allowed: bool, probe: &mut dyn Probe, missing: &mut bool) -> Result<bool, crate::error::RuntimeError> {
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::Public { .. } | RuleExpr::Allow => Ok(true),
        RuleExpr::Deny => Ok(false),
        RuleExpr::Eq(left, right) => Ok(term(left, viewer, old, next, probe, missing)? == term(right, viewer, old, next, probe, missing)?),
        RuleExpr::Ne(left, right) => Ok(term(left, viewer, old, next, probe, missing)? != term(right, viewer, old, next, probe, missing)?),
        RuleExpr::Compare(op,left,right) => {
            let left = term(left, viewer, old, next, probe, missing)?;
            let right = term(right, viewer, old, next, probe, missing)?;
            let (Value::Int(a),Value::Int(b)) = (left,right) else { return Ok(false); };
            Ok(match op { crate::ir::BinaryOp::Gte => a >= b, crate::ir::BinaryOp::Gt => a > b,
                crate::ir::BinaryOp::Lte => a <= b, crate::ir::BinaryOp::Lt => a < b, _ => false })
        }
        RuleExpr::And(_) | RuleExpr::Or(_) | RuleExpr::Not(_) => unreachable!("compound rule"),
        RuleExpr::Exists { table, index, key, equalities, .. } | RuleExpr::Created { table, index, key, equalities, .. } => {
            // Bind each operand once, through one codec/allocation path for both leaves.
            let created = matches!(rule, RuleExpr::Created { .. });
            if created && !created_allowed {
                return Err(crate::error::RuntimeError::Schema("created probes are valid only in insert rules".into()));
            }
            let key = key.iter().map(|term| self::term(term, viewer, old, next, probe, missing)).collect::<Result<Vec<_>, _>>()?;
            if *missing { return Ok(false); }
            if created {
                if !probe.created(table, index, &key) { return Ok(false); }
                if equalities.is_empty() { return Ok(true); }
            }
            let equalities = equalities.iter().map(|(column, value)| Ok((column.clone(), term(value, viewer, old, next, probe, missing)?))).collect::<Result<Vec<_>, crate::error::RuntimeError>>()?;
            if *missing { return Ok(false); }
            probe.exists(table, index, &key, &equalities)
        }
    }
}

pub(super) fn term(term: &RuleTerm, viewer: &str, old: Option<&Value>, next: &Value, probe: &mut dyn Probe, missing: &mut bool) -> Result<Value, crate::error::RuntimeError> {
    Ok(match term {
        RuleTerm::Field(field) | RuleTerm::NextField(field) => { probe.field(field, false); next.field(field).cloned().unwrap_or(Value::Null) },
        RuleTerm::OldField(field) => { probe.field(field, true); old.and_then(|old| old.field(field).ok()).cloned().unwrap_or(Value::Null) },
        RuleTerm::Reference { column, old: previous, .. } | RuleTerm::ReferenceChain { column, old: previous, .. } => {
//! Referential actions on delete (LLP 3000.039 §3): before a delete removes
//! its row, reserve a schema-fixed charge, close over the `cascade`
//! references, refuse while a `restrict` reference still holds a closure row
//! from outside the closure, clear `set null` references outside it, and
//! remove the closure children first. The caller then removes the row itself.
// @ref LLP 3000.039#3 — closure first; probes exclude the closure; deletion dominates set null.
use crate::engine::runner::Runner;
use crate::{
    error::{MeterKind, RuntimeError},
    meter::{Ceilings, Cut, Meter},
    rows::{Bounds, Dir},
    schema::{Effect, references::{OnDelete, Referencing}},
    value::Value,
};
use std::collections::BTreeSet;

impl Runner<'_> {
    pub(super) fn apply_reference_actions(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let schema = self.schema;
        if !schema.is_referenced(table_name) { return Ok(()); }
        let Some(price) = schema.delete_reservation(table_name) else {
            // A device without a reached child's exported price cannot charge
            // what the server charges; the server always has every price.
            if self.device { return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(format!("deleting {table_name} needs every referencing table's write price; queued without a local delete"))); }
            return Err(RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{table_name}'s delete actions have no reservation: a cascading reference lacks its cap or its child's write price (guide writes)")));
        };
        // @ref LLP 3000.039#4.2 — what the commit publishes is fixed by the schema, not by the rows the actions reach.
        let closure = schema.action_tables(table_name);
        for table in &closure.written {
            if schema.visible(table) { self.written_tables.insert(table.clone()); }
            self.action_tables.insert(table.clone());
        }
        for table in &closure.read { self.read_tables.insert(table.clone()); }
        if !closure.written.is_empty() { self.tx.widen_change_evidence(); }
        for (kind, amount) in [(MeterKind::Steps, price[0]), (MeterKind::IndexEntries, price[1]), (MeterKind::Examined, price[2]), (MeterKind::RowsWritten, price[3]), (MeterKind::OutputBytes, price[5]), (MeterKind::RuleProbes, price[6])] {
            if amount > 0 { self.meter.charge(kind, amount)?; }
        }
        // Pending event images survive the closure and subsequent statements.
        // Keep their schema-fixed reservation until the request ends.
        let events = schema.delete_event_heap(table_name).unwrap_or(0);
        self.meter.heap(events)?;
        let mark = self.meter.mark();
        self.meter.heap(price[4].saturating_sub(events))?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.039#3 — draw the reservation's rows from the request's physical allowance before the first seek.
        if !self.context.is_system() { self.tx.draw(table_name, price[2])?; }
        let meter = std::mem::replace(&mut self.meter, Meter::new(Ceilings::unbounded(), Cut::Abort));
        let prepaid = self.tx.reference_prepaid(true);
        let action = std::mem::replace(&mut self.engine_action, true);
        let result = self.reference_closure(table_name, old);
        self.engine_action = action;
        self.tx.reference_prepaid(prepaid);
        self.meter = meter;
        self.meter.release_heap(mark);
        result
    }

    /// Steps 3–6. The walk holds identities, never images: each child scan
    /// streams `PAGE` rows per seek, and every write rereads its row current.
    fn reference_closure(&mut self, root_table: &str, root: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let schema = self.schema;
        let mut seen: BTreeSet<(String, String)> = BTreeSet::from([(root_table.to_owned(), root.field("id")?.as_str()?.to_owned())]);
        // (Vectors, not maps: the web device pays for every new collection type.)
        let mut visits: Vec<(String, Value)> = vec![(root_table.to_owned(), root.field("id")?.clone())];
        // 3. Close over cascades, breadth first. Every reached row's referencing groups must be held on a device.
        let mut next = 0;
        while next < visits.len() {
            let (table, id) = visits[next].clone();
            next += 1;
            for r in schema.referencing(&table) {
                if self.device && !through_reference(&r) { self.prove_reference_held(&r, &id)?; }
                if r.action != OnDelete::Cascade { continue; }
                self.each_child(&r, &id, true, &mut |_, child| {
                    if seen.insert((r.child.to_owned(), child.as_str()?.to_owned())) { visits.push((r.child.to_owned(), child)); }
                    Ok(true)
                })?;
            }
        }
        // 4. Restrict: every probe runs, streaming past closure rows (the root
        // included) to the first outside witness; the first occupied reference
        // in schema order is reported. One reference's probes visit each closure
        // row at most once, plus one witness per probe (LLP 3000.039 §5.1).
        let mut occupied: Option<(String, String, String)> = None;
        for (table, id) in &visits {
            for r in schema.referencing(table) {
                if r.action != OnDelete::Restrict || through_reference(&r) { continue; }
                let mut outside = false;
                self.each_child(&r, id, false, &mut |_, child| {
                    outside = !seen.contains(&(r.child.to_owned(), child.as_str()?.to_owned()));
                    Ok(!outside)
                })?;
                let candidate = (r.child.to_owned(), r.column.to_owned(), table.clone());
                if outside && occupied.as_ref().is_none_or(|o| candidate < *o) { occupied = Some(candidate); }
            }
        }
        if let Some((child, column, parent)) = occupied {
            #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
            self.refusing_clause(&child, &format!("{column}: {parent} (on delete restrict)"));
            let via = if parent == root_table { String::new() } else { format!(", through a {parent} row this delete cascades to") };
            return Err(RuntimeError::Structural { code: "E_REFERENCED", message: format!("{root_table} at {} is still referenced through {child}.{column} (on delete restrict){via}; delete or retarget those rows first, or declare the reference's action (guide writes)", self.statement_site) });
        }

                }
            }

        })?;
        if predecessor.as_ref().is_none_or(|old| self.expired(table, old)) && has_children {

                // disclosed by the collision's logical meter or heap peak.
                self.meter.restore(mark);

                    RuntimeError::Constraint(format!("{table_name} already has a row with id {id}"))

        if let Some(existing) = &predecessor {

    pub(super) fn update(&mut self, table_name: &str, key: &Value, patch: Supplied) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {

    }

    fn update_attempt(&mut self, table_name: &str, key: &Value, patch: Supplied) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.refuse_engine_owned(table_name, Effect::Update)?;

        super::sequences::refuse_fields(&self.schema.tables[table_name], table_name, &patch)?;

            return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{table_name} update patch cannot change id")));
        }

## Summary

Snapback 4 is one of two parallel candidates for the successor to Snapback 2,
and the sibling of Snapback 3, not its successor. It builds
[LLP 2000.000](./2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md) as
written: the Snapback 2 core kept verbatim, two substrates flipped up front —
resources metered at runtime instead of priced statically, and a client that
replicates the viewer's declared partition instead of caching receipted
results — proved first on the hub fixture, with a deterministic simulator as
the primary harness. Snapback 3 ([LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md), led by
Codex / GPT-6 Astra) keeps the same two questions open and discovers their
answers in its first slice. Snapback 2 ([LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md))
continues as the incumbent and the control. All three are judged by one
pre-registered comparison on one clock.

This document records the author's decisions of 2026-09-12 that created the
track, restates the bet on one page so a reader can orient without the brief,
fixes the bakeoff protocol, draws the fence, and plans the first four weeks.
The design argument and its evidence stay in LLP 2000.000. Only running code
will bind (`rules/RULES.md`).

## 1. Decisions (author, 2026-09-12)

On 2026-09-12 Charlie Cheever asked whether Claude Fable 5.1 should lead a
Snapback 4 while Codex / GPT-6 Astra leads Snapback 3 and Snapback 2
continues, "and see which one turns out best." The lead answered yes with
conditions; the author accepted them ("Sounds good") and asked for this
document. The decisions below are therefore the lead's proposal as accepted
by the author. Where the author has not separately ruled, the text says so.

1. **Three tracks, one comparison.** Snapback 2 continues as the incumbent
   and the control. Snapback 3 builds LLP 2000; Snapback 4 builds LLP
   2000.000. This is a bakeoff of *bets* — discover the substrates in the
   slice versus decide them first — not of models. The result will say more
   about the bets than about the leads, and §3 is written so that it can.
2. **The name is Snapback 4.** Siblings, not a sequence: "4" does not mean
   "after 3." A separate number gives the track its own corpus root,
   directory, package identity and data directory with no rename when the
   bakeoff resolves, and files never move in LLP. If Snapback 4 loses, it is
   a number that never shipped. "Snapback 3b" was considered and rejected:
   it reads as a variant of 3, and this is a different bet.
3. **Snapback 4 builds LLP 2000.000 as written.** Its §14 open questions are
   the lead's to decide inside the track, recorded in §7 as they are
   decided. The author rules on the bar, the comparison and promotion (§3).
4. **One owner on the core.** The lead writes the engine, the store and the
   client contract. Codex lanes build the fixture generator, the reference
   applications, the device witnesses and the periphery. Blind reviews from
   two model families are reserved for the two disclosure-sensitive
   mechanisms: partition inversion of read rules (LLP 2000.000 §7.1) and
   the meter's cut behaviour on non-uniform scans (LLP 2000.000 §6). This
   departs from the standing rule that Codex codes and Claude orchestrates,
   deliberately, for the core only.
5. **The clock is four weeks** from the first commit under `snapback4/` to
   the first slice running on the hub fixture. At the clock the author kills
   the track, merges its substrates into a survivor, or continues it. There
   is no third month without a ruling.
6. **Shared and not shared.** Shared with Snapback 3: the LLP corpus, the
   Clique harness and scorers, the fixtures, the briefs, and the simulator
   once either track has one. Not shared: code, except the Snapback 2 pieces
   both are expected to copy (LLP 2000.000 §5.1).
7. **Snapback 2 keeps shipping and freezes new substrate work for the
   duration.** Fix-forward, the loop bar, acquisition and papercuts continue;
   no new certificate axes, client custody mechanisms or ceiling changes.
   This is accepted here as a condition of the bakeoff. Making it binding on
   concurrent Snapback 2 sessions needs one line in `rules/NOT-DOING.md`'s
   Moves ledger, which this document does not write.
8. **Reading load.** This root, updated in place, and one append-only
   journal, `snapback4/JOURNAL.md`. Sub-LLPs only for a decision that must
   outlive the journal. No panels beyond decision 4's two reviews.
